  I\   What are the elel'lflnts of fraud?
  i
 )     Deceit and fraud, if not acted upon, or if not accompanied-by inJury,
         p moral, not legal wrongs, It is of the very essance or an action
 1
  •    li•!i L&& bi deceit that the same shall 'be accompanied 'bv da'1\ll.""•
             In a suit for rencission of a contract for tho purchase of len~
 }     upon the groULd of a fraudulent oral m1arepresentation of an existing
       material fact affecting the value of the land, the fe.c.t about o;hich
 I     the misrepresentation is made must be one the true nature of which
       is not capable of being ascertained by the exercise of reasonable effort
       on the part of the one to whom the misrepresentation is made,
         Stokes et al. v. Victory Land eo., 1!9 Fla, 751! 0 quoted by Buford, j, in
       Stephens v, Coon, 177 So, 1111 1 Fla. 11137.
 l
 !
 •     Whet no the courts generally say of trat:d?

       As a rUle freud is never presumed, We have said that the rroof must be
       clear, cogent, convincing, positive, and satisfactory. We have said that
       the evidence of fraud must preponderate to the degree of overcoming all
       opposing evidence and repelling all presunption of good faith, r.bere a
       transaction is fairly susceptible of two constructions the one y;hich will
       free it of the imputation of fraud will be adopted, Likewise, when fraud
       is alleged, it must be proved and cannot bo inferred from facts consistent
       with honesty of purpose,
         Bayless, Brotherhood of Railroad Trainmen v, Br~~-, 71 r, 2nd 742


       1.\let there be damage in order for there to be fraud?

       Damage is an element or fraud in the law, He only who has trusted in and
       acted upon a falsehood to hie injury can maintain an action of fraud,
       Deception, which does not cause loae 1 is not fraud in the legal sense,
         Allen, C,J., Record v. Rochester Trust Co,, 192 A, 127, N.H. 1937


       Is fraud ever presumed?

       Fraud is never presumed, but rouet be proved by clear and satisfactory
       evidence by the party asserting it, and if the teats and circumstances
       from which the alleged fraud is supposed to arise may ~easonably co~sist
       with honest intentione, it will not be imputed,
         Allen v, Riddle, 141 Ala, 521 0 quoted by t~rgnn, C,J, in Green v, Buick, 7,
       P. 2nd 85, Idaho 1937


       Why is fraud never presumed?

       Fraud is neTer to be presupposed, but must be established by con~ncing proo
·'·    bY +.he greater weight of the evidence, as men are presumed to be bor.est an~
       their dealings with one another are assumed to be just and without taint.
         Byerly y, Byerly, 363 IIIo 517. Quoted by Riess, j, Beery y, Hurd,
       N.X. 2d 665, III. 11138

                               For record purposes, Mr. Joseph Maguire, General Counsel T
            of &•· Food and Drug Division of the Department of Health, Education and           ·)
            WelJ.·re, advised Mr. McGuire of my office that he understood recently a
            Dotlr,r iviichaehilvert of New York City called at the j~ew York Office
            repc•·ting that United States Attorney Peter-Mills, Mr. Josephvvtaguire, and
            one rhoma~anr:ravit"! w>:!re guilty of perjury in connection with the trial
            of Sl!>'ert, '''ilhcJ.r '''";~h, and the \Vilhelm Reich Foundation in the contempt
            of Cr·urt Tn<u he.tu m reaeral District Court, Portland, Maine, May 2 through
            May I, 1956, in which Silvert and others were co-defendants. Mr. Maguire
            advi ed that he understood our New York Office advised our Boston Office
            as h· had received this information concerning Silvert' s visit from United
            State" Attorney Peter Mills in Maine. What concerned Mr. Maguire was
            whet' :'3r Thomas Mangravite actually was also named along with himself
            and Peter Mills. Mangravite, an employee of the Reich Foundation, eventually
            beca ne a government witness and just as a follow-through, Maguire wanted to bee
            sure whether there was an indication if Mangravite was now back in the fold
            with Reich and Silvert. Maguire advised that on May 25, 1956, Wilhelm Reich
            was found guilty and given a two years sentence. Silvert was found guilty and
            give•· a year and a day, and the corporation was fined $10,000.

                             On checking the file we found that the New York Office reported
            by letter to the Bureau that Doctor Michael Silvert, one William'Moise of
